Every phase opens with a drift check against efe123c and closes green (build, build:pwa, vitest, shots where UI changes, review pass, recipes and capture with verify:board, PERSIST_VERSION when the seed changes, Decisions log updated for superseded rulings, a PROGRESS entry with its owner-review list).

How a phase runs
  1. Get the owner decisions first. D1 to D11 (the table at the top of ROADMAP.md) each gate one phase. A phase whose answer has not come in builds the stated default and labels it provisional.
  2. Pick the next phase from the table below, respecting the order in "Sequencing and rules". The Contracts track (17 to 25) and the Schedule track (28 to 32) each run strictly in order.
  3. Open a fresh Claude Code session and paste that phase's kick-off prompt. The agent reads the roadmap, its phase doc, the gap detail for its items, the prototype maps and PROGRESS.md before touching anything.
  4. Drift check first. The catalogue keeps moving. Each session diffs its items against fbdd555, reports what changed, and adapts the plan before building.
  5. The agent plans and builds straight on. It turns the phase doc into work-sized steps and starts building without waiting for approval, so phases can run back to back (ROADMAP.md "Owner review: agents test themselves").
  6. Every phase ends the same way: npm run build, npm run build:pwa, npx vitest run and (for UI work) npm run shots green; the checklist run and reported item by item; the adversarial review-and-fix pass (PROGRESS convention 18); the demo guide patched for any beat it broke; the catalogue screenshots brought in step with the app (the phase's capture recipes created or updated, then a full npm run capture in requirements-board/ with no failed recipe and npm run verify:board green, PROGRESS convention 19); a PROGRESS.md entry. Agents never commit.
  7. When the catalogue changes, follow README.md: re-grade the changed items, merge them into gaps.json, update the affected phase docs, and re-render this page with node requirements-board/agent/skills/update-build-plan/tools/build-index.mjs.

Demo buttons live on their screens. Anything that cannot be shown through normal use (scheduled jobs, backend events, external systems) gets a trigger in the harness bar's "Demo actions" menu, listed only on the screen where it matters. The installed PWA has no harness bar, so it gets the same list as a badged demo-actions sheet, plus office stand-ins for beats that wait on the office. Phase 14 builds the mechanism; every later phase registers into it.

The checklists are the contract. Each phase ends with a short manual test checklist. The agent runs it itself in the running app and reports each item with evidence; nothing is handed to the owner per phase. Each PROGRESS entry ends with a "For the owner's review" list, and Phase 44 gathers them into one list for a single review once every catch-up phase is done.

What each milestone can show
  • After 16 Built. Booking vocabulary everywhere, with Copy a Booking gone, photo capture out of the Add a booking chooser and an assigned List called ACTIVE. Every demo action on its own screen, in the harness bar and on a handset. Warnings, never blocks: one routine, the to-do list, a triangle on Bookings, the prepayment gate turned into a warning, no confirm step at submit. The payable equals the receivable, AA's fee is a monthly invoice, and Xero holds no patient names.
  • After 38a The office's schedule (28 to 32 built, finished by 32a and 32b): every session stored and free by default over a configurable horizon, Slots and Lists, statuses as a user-maintained list, the availability calendar, recurring bookings, Draft Lists assigned by the office with preferences and tiers and never onto an unavailable session; unavailability, sickness included, forcing a return or hand-on and never a conflict, the conflict dashboard showing hospital closures; whoever moves a List choosing what the vacated session becomes; Lists handed on and single Bookings moved into a free colleague's session as a new List, colleagues listed by availability alone; the notification pool with notices ticked resolved and alerts from a List or Booking; and the anaesthetist changing a submitted List until it is authorised, a main view from today and a search for past work. S2 is re-scripted.
  • After 25 An out-of-hours List after the PM session (32c, a working design), then the catalogue's flat Contract model end to end: Parties (no insurer master) with billing details and delivery by email or post, and Contracts as one priced item each (AA code and holder reference code, bills its holder or the payer, links, prepaid, five pricing kinds, the override on RVG kinds only, Active or Disabled, edited in place with an audit trail, never deleted) with No contract · RVG held by AA; AA's specialties and funding tags (SXAP, HNZ, ACC) on surgeons and Contracts and an open-price fallback for every invoice destination; RVG groups from the NZSA guide with the range shown and an RVG code by type-ahead; every schedule item a fixed-price Contract with backdatable price ranges, combinations as Contracts, prepaid price lists above zero, each Contract's invoice description and default RVG code, starting values from the RVG group; optional itemised modifiers, age and P1 pre-applied and removable; one Contract per Procedure through the decided step rail (where the invoice goes, Payment type or the hospital's tag, the Contract by typing, No contract · RVG pinned first or 'Other price'; 'Needs an invoice destination'; the funder's path 'Suggested', never narrowing), changeable by the anaesthetist until AUTHORISED, never onto or off prepaid; one procedure text the hospital's replaces with the original in the history, and the invoice description by its defaults, an admin's edit always winning; the payer on the Booking, who is billed by the Contract, the insurer or funder and approval number (a warning for insurers only); the form following the pricing kind, completion as three conditions and office review flags; invoices in the anaesthetist's name by the Party's delivery or to post, with the Split; the RVG multi-procedure rule on RVG-priced Procedures; five pricing kinds with a price following its Contract until held, a backdated range reaching only unheld Procedures, the anaesthetist seeing what the price comes to and overriding only where allowed, the office changing any price with no reason; and a lock at AUTHORISED that reproduces invoices, with each Contract's history. S1 Beat 3, S2 Beat 2, S3, S4 Beat 3 and S5 are re-scripted.
  • After 27a Prepayment started per Procedure by a Contract under Payment type: Prepaid (a price-list Contract, or Prepaid · Fixed price with a typed amount), invoiced to the payer: the amount is the Procedure's price when the invoice is raised, in full and shown to the anaesthetist, who can never change it, and read-only for the office too once raised; the invoice generated when an admin approves it (a step AA can switch off), its payable held in the AA Holding account; part-paid tracking and a warning that strengthens as the date nears, beside the balance alert; at authorisation the prepaid Procedure priced at its amount and left off the final invoice with nothing raised automatically, and a transfer moving the money to whoever did the List. S4 Beat 1 is re-scripted.
  • After 35 S1 rebuilt: both first-release intake sources on one review-and-merge screen, one theatre list a day from the two integrated hospitals and the rooms' PDF lists uploaded, corrected and sent to matching (a re-sent List showing its differences), each row's procedure text as received, a hospital's text replacing the rooms' (the original in the Booking's change history) and the invoice description unless an admin edited it, rows without an NHI waiting with a stub in their place on the List until the office adds the NHI, a day move as a cancellation plus a new Booking, then a Booking on 'Needs an invoice destination' whose Contract the office sets through the decided step rail (Patient › Standard: No contract · RVG, the RVG code handed to the anaesthetist). Explicit save with change sets, a change history screen on each Booking naming who changed what (office users by name, or the hospital's list), and the on-demand update email from the change history, from a template, to the rooms (an empty To where a room holds no email; the main room's for a surgeon at more than one) or the hospital, including from a List-move notice. Surgeons' rooms held with no email, surgeons at more than one room, and practice manager and PA contacts on the surgeon's profile (17a).
  • After 39a The money story on the internal ledger: balances in or out of balance, the AA Holding account's legs and transfers, payments to anaesthetists made in Xero's payables run and detected by the engine, Xero mirror resilience and a processing monitor grouped by anaesthetist, ledger-backed web accounts with a flat outstanding list and a monthly GST report of payments made, events billed in the next run (bundled with the same party's invoice where possible) with additional invoices for office and anaesthetist, the Booking total on the office's Booking page with each Procedure's price and events, the credit note option, credit-and-rebill from copied lines and a combination split after sending that equals the credit, and weekly ISO-week payment runs with BCTI approval, netting and a remittance advice. S4 Beats 2 to 5 are re-scripted.
  • After 44 Every verified gap closed but the three parked units: pre-op, post-op and billing events on every Procedure (no billing lines, on no List), patients with a mandatory NHI, intake rows waiting for one with their stubs on the List resolved from the waiting view, balance warnings for the office and on the anaesthetist's List, follow-up tools and shared notes, NHI lookup, the prepayment letter, by-hand differences on prepaid work with part credits, a cancelled prepayment (or a prepaid Contract changed after sending) credited or refunded from the AA Holding account automatically, a moved prepaid Booking changing nothing, reference-data loads (Parties, specialties, funding tags and priced Contracts with their ranges) with surgeons at more than one room, full-scale and privacy demos at about 28,000 invoices a year, anaesthetist sign-in, point-of-need help, the office's help desk panel, and the rewritten S1 to S5 run sheet. Still parked: the A and B split of a session and the pre-op consult in AA's room.
Parked 3
  • US-01.1.5 Proposed (new 5 November): split a session into an A and a B List, Donald's working idea from Vanessa's request, not designed or agreed (Greg argued against splitting on 19 October). The catalogue keeps one List per Slot (US-01.1.1, Agreed) until the split is agreed, so building it would contradict an agreed rule across the grid, generation, recurring painting, availability, moves, conflicts and the finders (about 36 files read the session). Unparks into a phase after 32c once AA agrees it and says how the halves' times, a half handed to another anaesthetist and a List already holding Bookings work.
  • DM-65 The data-model delta for US-01.1.5 (Slot to List becomes one to one-or-two with an A/B label); the delta itself says not to build it until the split is agreed. Parked with US-01.1.5.
  • US-03.7.4 Proposed (new 5 November): bill a pre-op consult held in AA's room, on OQ-138, which has no recommendation and leaves who is billed (the patient, or the anaesthetist for the room), how it is priced and whether it ties to a Procedure open, so there is no default to build. Unparks into 39b (as an event kind) or 38b (as an additional invoice) once AA describes the case. It is not the ACC pre-op assessment, which 39b builds.
About this plan

The prototype was built in July against the original RFP, in fourteen phases. The requirements catalogue has since become the source of truth and moved a long way: Bookings instead of Cards, Slots and Draft Lists, one Contract per Procedure locked at authorise, admin-decided hospital matching, an internal ledger with Xero as its mirror. This plan closes every verified gap in 50 further phases, numbered 14 to 44, each one focused session (at most two) with one coherent deliverable, and every automatic or external event given a demo button on the screen where it matters.

The prototype is the July RFP model with Phases 15 to 32 layered on. Calendar, Slots, Lists, Draft Lists, pairings, tiers, warnings engine, the AA fee run and the Xero simulation are close to the catalogue. Money and intake are not. Of roughly 235 graded items, about 40 Match, about 25 are Partial, about 15 are Missing, and the rest are Contradicts or Grouping. Contradicts dominates EP-02 to EP-08, EP-11 and EP-12: the prototype implements a different, superseded design there, not a smaller version of the new one. EP-01, EP-10 and most of EP-13 are near done. The core is one coordinated rework: Contract (holder, dated versions, lines, flags) replaces billing route plus Type 1/2/3. Pricing, completion, prepayment, invoicing, payer, review and events all hang off it. Statuses were not used to scope work; they appear below only where an open question (OQ) makes a gap unsafe.

Plan baseline fbdd555. The full plan is ROADMAP.md, the gap analysis GAP-ANALYSIS.md, the data plan.json.

Rendered by build-index.mjs from plan.json and status.json, plan baseline fbdd555.
